### 2026-09-14 — Customer PSD protection warning traced (10.0.3)
Codex investigated Dave Ward's screenshot: SOURCE PAINT PROTECTED, 76/104 expected pixel layers decoded, failure noticed with Restrict to Layers. The banner is persistent import-safety UI, not a base-color-mode warning. Import falls back on unsupported group semantics or a materially divergent reconstructed composite; the decoded count alone does not identify which trigger fired. Fallback preserves the locked visible source composite and marks original layers hidden. The source-layer union excludes hidden/missing pixels, and rendering emits an empty restriction mask, explaining why a zone restricted to an original recovery layer can paint nothing. Exact PSD, affected layer name and import logs are needed to establish the 28 missing rasters' cause; no customer-file reproduction or importer fix claimed.
Verification: canvas controller, PSD safety helper and import routes match the packaged runtime by SHA-256; 10.0.3 changed only the base-color explicit flag in the canvas controller and did not alter this importer path. Executed the actual fallback and source-layer-union functions: locked composite retained, decoded recovery layer hidden, restricted union empty (PASS). Source-layer rebind/recipe round-trip Node suite PASS. PSD hierarchy suite 7 passed / 1 stale test failed because it searches for `_doPSDImport(psdPath)` while runtime uses `_doPSDImport(psdPath, importOptions)`; not evidence of the customer's failure. Runtime, release artifacts and Cloudflare untouched. Investigation lane closed; customer-specific cause remains unconfirmed.

### 2026-09-05 — Claude — BETA LAUNCH PREP 10.0.1: three owner fixes + the honest beta-update path

- **File Picker setting was a no-op in a browser tab (owner: "showing up in Settings but nothing changes").** Root cause: `'windows'` mode could only reach `dialog.showOpenDialog` through the Electron preload bridge; the owner runs the app at localhost:59876 in a browser, where `window.electronAPI` does not exist, so the bridge fell straight back to the Shokker Browser. Fix: the Flask server (same desktop) now opens the real Windows chooser itself — `POST /api/native-dialog` in `server_routes/file_picker_routes.py` runs an STA PowerShell child (WinForms OpenFileDialog for files, COM IFileOpenDialog with FOS_PICKFOLDERS for folders, result via UTF-8 temp file, one dialog at a time, 15-min cap, internal header required). `js/spb-native-file-dialogs.js`: Electron bridge first, server dialog second; the generic `window.openFilePicker` wrapper now honours the saved setting too; header tooltips flip with the setting. Failures toast, cancel never opens a second picker. **Verified live:** Settings→Windows, header 📂 → chooser opened by the server in 0.3 s, TGA picked → Source Paint box updated, status ✓.
- **Trap found on the way:** an invisible owner form parked at (-32000,-32000) put the chooser OFF-SCREEN and the request hung forever (Windows anchors a dialog to its owner). Owner now sits half a dialog up-left of screen centre. Also: a PowerShell `FindWindow` companion never found the dialog; ctypes `EnumWindows` by PID does — harnesses in the session scratchpad.
- **Version badge** (owner: "overlapping the iracing user id box… SMALL, above the red line, beside SHOKKER PAINT BOOTH"): the "red line" is the orange EKG `.header-brand::after` (gallery looks). New `css/spb-beta-polish-20260905.css` (linked BEFORE the clean skin, wins on specificity): 9 px gold text, absolute at left 138 / bottom 15 inside the 194 px brand reserve at ≥1350 px (measured right edge 179 vs User ID box at 212, 8 px above the EKG line in gallery look); in-flow 9 px under the 48 px logo below 1350 px (inside the 62 px header at 1100 px).
- **BASE COLOR lock box** (owner: "JACKED UP AGAIN… a freaking square box", third regression): every look restyles `input[type=checkbox]` globally (pro-theme dark-on-dark, clean skin native 12 px), so styling the input always lost. The visible square is now a `.spb-lock-box` SPAN (14 px, 2 px accent border, fills + check when checked); the real checkbox stays 1×1/transparent for state, `onchange`, keyboard. Same file. Verified 14×14 at 1920/PRO.
- **Shipped:** root + `electron-app/server` mirror (new CSS added to `scripts/runtime-sync-manifest.json`), `?v=` bumped for the bridge and state-zones, server refreshed via supervisor (gen 9, PID 78028; registry READY identical to gen 8). Picker groups 29 → 23. Full record: [`docs/RETIRED_SHELVES_2026-09-05.md`](docs/RETIRED_SHELVES_2026-09-05.md).

**HOW, and the distinction that matters.** Removed the six entries from `BASE_GROUPS` ONLY. Every one of the 104 `BASES` entries and every engine renderer was left untouched, and verified after the change: **104/104 still render** with finite paint and spec. Retiring a shelf must never repaint a customer's saved car — hiding them stops anyone *choosing* one; deleting them would silently change every design that already used one. Reversal is a one-line restore of the six group blocks.

**THE TRAP: 17 places the app itself pointed at a retired id.** Found before touching anything, and repointed first, so nothing was left aimed at a hidden finish:
`metallic`→`f_metallic` (6 presets), `chrome`→`f_chrome` (5 presets + the spec-preview tab), `candy`→`f_candy`, `pearl`→`f_pearl` (2), `carbon_base`→`wrap_twill_film`, `brushed_titanium`→`f_brushed`, `ceramic`→`wrap_ceramic_coat` (Easy Mode quick list), and `piano_black` dropped from that list as redundant beside `gloss` and `wet_look`. Verified after: **zero remaining base references to any retired id**.

**Why that check was necessary:** the 104 included the most generic ids in the catalog — `chrome`, `metallic`, `pearl`, `candy`, `ceramic`, `piano_black`, `copper`, `platinum`. Grepping for the id alone gives hundreds of false hits (the WORD "chrome" is everywhere in tags and search maps). The only reliable filter was matching `base:`/`data-base=` VALUE positions, which cut ~489 raw hits down to the 17 that were real.

**Cache tokens bumped on all three changed files** (`0-finish-data`, `6-ui-boot`, `2-state-zones`) — a JS edit without its own `?v=` bump does not reach the app.

### 2026-09-04 — Claude — FOUR new shelves shipped (64 finishes) + a 64-agent burn to learn from

**Live and gate-clean: 🎨 THE BOOTH (16), 🕵️ MULE (16), ⚡ LIGHTNING SHOKK (16), 🐍 SLITHERIN (16).**
Every shelf: **FINISH LAW 0 failures**, **UNIQUENESS 16/16**, all under 3s at 2048, thumbnails baked and distinct, token `spb-four-shelves-20260904e`, all confirmed in `/api/finish-data`. Catalog is now 1186 bases.

**⚠️ THE MISTAKE WORTH READING — I ran a 64-wide agent fleet and it produced NOTHING.**
Four shelves were launched as four concurrent 16-agent workflows. All 64 agents hit the session limit, ~7.1M subagent tokens were spent, and **zero finishes came back**. CLAUDE.md's token mandate says *"Fleet size = burn rate. Run agent lanes in waves (≤3 concurrent for authoring jobs)"* — written after a NINE-wide fleet did the same thing. I ran seven times that. All 64 finishes were then authored directly, incrementally, one shelf at a time, and every shelf was written to disk and gated before the next began.

**🔴 THE FINISH LAW'S SCALE WINDOW IS RESOLUTION-DEPENDENT — RUN IT AT `--res 2048`.**
`scale_axis` computes `r_fine = n / (8/2048*n)` — the `n` CANCELS, so the annulus is fixed at FFT radius 64-256 whatever the render size. Feature size is `n/r`, so:
* at **n=2048** the window is the intended **8-32px**;
* at the default **n=1024** it is actually **4-16px**.
THE BOOTH measured **6 failures at the default 1024 and only 3 at 2048** — the same finishes, judged against a window half the intended size. Always pass `--res 2048`, or a correct shelf gets rejected for features it was supposed to have.

**What each shelf is, and why it exists**
* **THE BOOTH** — paint-shop defects: fisheye, solvent pop, sags, dry spray, mottling, tiger stripe, die-back, blush, lifting, sanding telegraph, dirt nibs, edge mapping, buff holograms, mask bleed, tape ridge, water spot. The app's own subject and the emptiest domain in the catalog (fisheye/die-back/sanding-telegraph/buff-holograms all measured **0** across 4035 finishes). Every one MODULATES the painter's colour.
* **MULE** — automotive prototype disguise, deliberately NOT military camo (woodland/MARPAT/DPM/Kryptek/dazzle already exist and were ruled off limits). Erlkonig swirl, cladding, false shutlines, moire tuned to alias a sensor, countershading that cancels form, retroreflective flash traps.
* **LIGHTNING SHOKK** — sixteen DISCHARGE MORPHOLOGIES. The catalog had 59 lightning-named and 205 electric-named finishes but only **5** that were actually a branching discharge STRUCTURE; the rest are electric-COLOURED, not electric-SHAPED. Every finish here is a FIELD of discharge (branch spacing 12-24px) because a single hero bolt is a macro composition that fails SCALE outright.
* **SLITHERIN** — sixteen snakes by SCALE ARCHITECTURE, against the stiffest existing competition of anything built today (84 snake-named + 30 reptile-scale finishes already present). Keeled vs smooth, overlapping cycloid vs non-overlapping granular, ventral scutes, stretched hood lozenges, papery shed imprints. Pattern tones derive from the painter's base colour.

**Recurring faults caught by eye that every gate passed**
1. **Regular lattices reading as polka dots.** Fisheye and dirt nib both came out as neat grids — contamination does not land on a grid. Full-cell jitter + per-instance radius + a keep test fixed both, and stopped them twinning each other.
2. **A spec channel reading a DIFFERENT field than its paint.** mask bleed 0.13, tape ridge -0.105, spark gap 0.271, sunbeam 0.154 — all four were one or two channels driven off a side-field instead of the paint's own modulation. FOLLOW is constructed, never argued.
3. **Hue rotation breaking the shelf's colour rule.** Milk snake turned a RED car GREEN. A shelf that promises "derive tones from the base" cannot use a free hue rotate.
4. **A per-cell coin flip is confetti.** Gaboon chose its motif per cell and read as noise; choosing per BAND made it a snake.

### 2026-09-04 — Claude — 🔬 FLAW LAB shipped (25, NEW category) + WRAP SHOP 17 → 25
### 2026-09-04 — Claude — 🔬 FLAW LAB shipped (25, NEW category) + WRAP SHOP 17 → 25

**Two shelves live. Both gate-clean. 50 finishes added today.**

**🔬 FLAW LAB (25, new) — non-destructive testing: the invisible made visible.**
- FINISH LAW **0 failures / 25**, story ratio **1.00** — every finish owns its material story; no shelf has hit 1.00 before. UNIQUENESS **25/25** (nearest 34-48% vs an 80% line). All **under 3s at 2048**. 25 distinct thumbnails. Token `spb-flawlab-20260904d`. Live in `/api/finish-data`.
- **The domain was chosen by MEASUREMENT.** Scanning all 4010 base finishes across 20 candidate domains: NDT/inspection returned **1 hit** (a false positive) vs **159 for ice/frost** and **79 for lattice/foam**. Both "obviously novel" instincts — ice and TPMS lattices — were among the MOST covered things in the catalog. Measure before building.
- Techniques: dye penetrant, magnetic particle, isochromatic + isoclinic photoelasticity, moire deflectometry, shearography, holographic interferometry, C-scan, A-scan, phased array, radiography, CT, eddy current, TSA, flash thermography, acoustic emission, strain rosettes, brittle lacquer, Chladni, schlieren, shadowgraph, crack-tip fields, Barkhausen, macro-etch, Vickers traverse.
- **50 agents authored it against `_authoring_contract.md`. Landing rate: 25/25 assembled, rendered and validated with ZERO broken on the first pass.** The contract is the durable artifact — it encodes every failure this session paid for.

**🎞 WRAP SHOP 17 → 25 (wave 2)** — Hex PPF, Twill Film, Truchet Knurl, Craze Net, Lens Array, Flow Line, Spiral Burnish, Overlap Ghost. Chosen for STRUCTURAL spread: the shelf now spans square + hex lattice, weave, tile-curve, cellular-edge, optical-displacement, curl-flow, log-spiral polar and integer layer-count. LAW **0/25**, UNIQUENESS **25/25**, all under 3s.

**THE SCALE GATE, EXACTLY** (read from `scripts/spb_finish_law.py`, not from prose):
`band = FFT power in the 8-32px annulus / total power`, floor **0.20**, and the five gold standards reach only **0.218-0.443**. Consequences: energy BELOW 8px is **dilution** (adding fine detail to rescue SCALE makes it WORSE); energy above 32px is dilution; and **a blur can never be the load-bearing mechanism** — every low-pass moves power across the 32px cutoff by definition. Put the dominant spectral peak at **12-24px**.

**FOLLOW cannot be argued, only constructed.** Four rounds of physically-correct reasoning about which way a groove "should" drive roughness scored -0.12, -0.47, -0.49, -0.62. Probing candidate drivers found it in one run. In `paint_v2` a spec fn CANNOT see the rendered paint (648 of 690 have no `paint` arg), so the only way to pass is to REBUILD the paint's field via a shared `_cache` key and drive all three channels off it. One channel reading a different field is enough to fail.

**A judge's line worth keeping**, on a rival proposal: *"this is 25 mechanisms and 4 looks ... the proposal's own defence ('different math') is the exact defence the owner banned, because the Uniqueness Law judges RENDERED STRUCTURE, not source."* Check every shelf for visual archetype collapse BY EYE.

**New tools:** `scripts/spb_variant_search.py` (the owner's "iterate 10x, pick the best" made mechanical + auditable), `scripts/spb_contact_sheet.py` (1:1 crops at 2048 — it caught 11 flat-grey finishes that all passed the search), `scripts/spb_assemble_shelf.py`, `engine/paint_v2/_finish_kit_2026.py`, `_authoring_contract.md`.

**Still open:** the six legacy shelves (Carbon, Ceramic & Glass, Chrome & Mirror, Metallic Standard, Exotic Metal, Candy & Pearl remainder) are NOT retired. `fl_phased_array` is the weakest of the 50 and would be the first to revisit.
